The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. MC	<p>ed5f_a644</p> <h5>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Mapping</h5><p>In this problem, you will use unordered tetrads to determine the between a single pair of genes and calculate the distances between them. The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #735b00;">Gene K</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #735b00;"><i>knotted</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #735b00;">Gene K</span> cells grow in twisted, coiled shapes, resulting in a knotted or gnarled appearance.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #00639b;">Gene M</span></strong> is correlated with the '<span style="color: #00639b;"><i>militant</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #00639b;">Gene M</span> colonies are small, dense, and secrete compounds that inhibit the growth of nearby colonies.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse; MC	<p>0503_194f</p> <h5>Unordered Tetrad Two Gene Mapping</h5><p>In this problem, you will use unordered tetrads to determine the between a single pair of genes and calculate the distances between them. The yeast <i>Saccharomyces cerevisiae</i> is used in this study. A cross has been performed to study the linkage relationships among two genes, and the resulting genotypes are summarized in the table below.</p><h6>Characteristics of Recessive Phenotypes</h6><p><ul><li><strong><span style="color: #82520a;">Gene D</span></strong> is connected with the '<span style="color: #82520a;"><i>doubled</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #82520a;">Gene D</span> cells display double or multiple budding, with several buds emerging simultaneously.</li> <li><strong><span style="color: #006d5e;">Gene F</span></strong> is affiliated with the '<span style="color: #006d5e;"><i>fuzzy</i></span>' phenotype. A budding yeast that is homozygous recessive for <span style="color: #006d5e;">Gene F</span> colonies are covered in soft, fine filaments, giving them a fuzzy, cotton-like texture.</li> </ul></p><table style="border-collapse: collapse;
